# MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R001` | **Bounded derivation steps in EL+/RL and tableau reasoning.** The EL+/RL and tableau reasoners enforce a derivation-step budget. They check consistency and classify the schema. They fail closed with a typed VALIDATION_BUDGET_EXCEEDED error instead of running without bound. Consistency checking reports no unsatisfiable classes on the shipped ontology corpus. | A unit test exhausts the budget and confirms the typed refusal. An integration test checks consistency across the full corpus. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R002` | **Rejection reasons stay out of the core kernel row schema.** When the engine rejects a query or validation, it reports the reason through the caller's log, metric or error channel. The engine adds no rejection field to the core kernel row structure. The kernel row format remains format-neutral and unchanged by the content of any given rejection reason. | A contract test confirms the kernel row schema carries no rejection-reason field. A negative test checks logs or error metadata for the rejection reason. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R003` | **Derivation-step budget is enforced once, not duplicated per path.** The derivation-step budget that bounds EL+/RL and tableau reasoning is implemented a single time. It applies to every reasoning entry point, including the path that composes and classifies the schema. This specification defines no separate step-budget enforcement here; that guarantee is owned by the typed-packs-and-catalog specification's schema composition work. | Covered by the step-budget enforcement test defined under the typed-packs-and-catalog specification; this specification requires no additional test for this item. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R004` | **Reasoning projection rebuilds after any skipped event.** If the reasoning projection misses or skips an update event, the engine triggers a full rebuild of the projection before serving any further reads from it, rather than serving results derived from an incomplete projection state. | An integration test injects a skipped projection event and confirms the engine rebuilds before serving reads. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R005` | **UQL `VALIDATE SHAPE` stage.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql`. Caller: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`src/server/handlers/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/dispatch/shape.rs::validate_shape` (shape dialect `SHACL` core through `medulla_shacl::validate_nodes`). The stage keeps conforming or violating rows per `ShapeKeep` and attaches a `ShapeReport` (R005.1). Order and scores stay unchanged. Refusals: `SHAPE_UNKNOWN` for an unnamed shape and `SHAPE_SOURCE_MISSING` when no `USING` document or graph shapes exist. Writes: none. Depends on: R005.700 for the integrity shapes, R005.1 for violation kinds. | Test: `tests/query/test_validate_shape_served.py::test_conforming_and_violating_rows` (L4). It serves a conforming and a violating fixture through `Uql`, asserts the kept rows and the report, and asserts `SHAPE_UNKNOWN`. A second tenant sees no rows. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R006` | **Proof-carrying results for `SPARQL` and rule-derived queries.** Stage: query execution, served `Sparql` and `Uql` with `PROOF`. Caller: served `Sparql` → `src/server/sparql_http.rs` handler → proof assembly `crates/medulla-plan/src/proof.rs::attach_proof`; and served `Uql` → `handle_uql` → `attach_proof`. Code: `crates/medulla-types/src/proof.rs` defines `Proof{premises, generation, rule_digests}` where each premise is a fact id plus revision. `generation` names the schema and index generation used. The response field is `proof`; the hand-written Python client preserves it unchanged. Refusal: `PROOF_GENERATION_STALE` when the serving generation differs from the checked one. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends on: MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R044. | Test: `tests/query/test_proof_served.py::test_sparql_and_rule_query_proofs` (L4). It requests a proof from a `Sparql` call and a rule-derived `Uql` call through the hand-written Python client. It re-checks each premise against the serving generation and asserts a stale generation refuses. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R007` | **Semantic-index reconciliation tombstones only on a complete proof.** Semantic-index reconciliation marks a source's entries as tombstoned only after an authoritative complete-snapshot proof confirms the source is genuinely missing; an interrupted or partial scan leaves the prior live entries untouched. When reconciliation refuses to proceed, it distinguishes an absent source from one whose proof is unissued or expired so the failure names the actual condition. | A regression test interrupts a source scan before the complete-snapshot proof and confirms no tombstone is created, then replays a complete scan and confirms exactly the absent sources are tombstoned. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R008` | **Edge-native text and vector search with stable identity.** The engine indexes and retrieves edges directly by text and vector similarity. It does not require edges to be reified into nodes. It uses a stable identity for parallel edges between the same pair of nodes. Searches apply tenant, purpose and row-level-security prefilters before candidate truncation. Index updates and deletes are safe to replay. The set of indexable targets is defined once and generated consistently into every client. | A correctness oracle compares filtered edge-search results against an unfiltered full scan, a replay test confirms idempotent update/delete behavior, and a generated-contract check confirms the target set matches across clients. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R009` | **Durable user-managed graph-index lifecycle.** A caller can create, check the status of, and drop a graph index through one durable, user-managed lifecycle that extends the engine's existing index-manager authority. Backfill is fenced against concurrent writes, activation is atomic, a blocked or partial index state is diagnosable, and the index recovers correctly after a restart, with the query planner falling back safely when a needed generation is unavailable. | An integration test exercises create, backfill, activate and drop plus a restart during backfill, and confirms atomic activation, diagnosable blocked state, and correct planner fallback. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R010` | **Tableau reasoning stays bounded on the shipped core schema.** Tableau consistency and classification reasoning over the shipped core schema completes within bounded time and memory. Schema-axiom internalization and backtracking cause no exponential blowup or stack overflow. Schema composition and restart replay that depend on this reasoning path complete successfully under normal resource limits. | A performance and fault test composes and classifies the full core schema under a restart-replay scenario and confirms it completes within declared time and memory budgets without overflow. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R011` | **EL+/RL reasoning is sound against the shipped ontology corpus.** The EL+/RL reasoner derives only sound subsumption and disjointness conclusions. A consistency check over the shipped core ontology corpus therefore reports no unsatisfiable classes. That includes owl:Nothing and its root class. The EL/RL and tableau reasoning paths agree on consistency and disjointness results over this corpus. | A full-corpus consistency regression test checks the shipped ontology for unsatisfiable classes. The test confirms that EL/RL and tableau results agree. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R012` | **Tableau honors AllDisjointClasses and core disjointness facts.** Tableau-based reasoning expands owl:AllDisjointClasses declarations the same way the EL path does, so schema-entry full-ABox checking enforces the core disjointness between information-content entities, generically-dependent continuants, processes and temporal regions, and between persons, organizations, servers and events. The corresponding fact that a taxon is never an organism is present and enforced. | An integration test checks that tableau reasoning rejects instances violating these disjointness constraints, including the taxon/organism fact. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R013` | **Foreign-source resolution is scoped to the owning principal.** Foreign sources use owner and actor keys. A caller with general federated-query access cannot use another principal's stored credentials. Central authorization conceals source identity. Unknown and known-but-ungranted names return the same typed not-found refusal. An exact sharing grant makes the source identity visible. An unqualified visible source name returns stable `ACCESS_DENIED`; use its qualified shared name. Foreign-source rows are not row-level-security filtered. Policy review must approve this clarification before R013 acceptance or native execution. | An integration test registers a foreign source for one principal. It confirms another principal receives the typed not-found response for an ungranted plain name. A second negative uses an existing exact sharing grant. It confirms stable `ACCESS_DENIED` for an unqualified visible name. R014 covers qualified access and revoke behavior. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R014` | **Explicit grant-based sharing of a registered foreign source.** A principal who owns a registered foreign source can share query access to it with another principal through the engine's existing role-based access-control grant mechanism, rather than through an implicit name lookup. The registering principal retains ownership of the source, and access can be granted and later revoked. | An integration test grants another principal access to a foreign source, confirms it can query through the grant, then revokes the grant and confirms the query is refused. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R015` | **Read-only enforcement at the shared SQL execution path.** The shared graph-free SQL execution path classifies and refuses DDL, DML, COPY and CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE statements as not read-only. That refusal lives once at the shared path instead of being duplicated per caller. Every caller that reaches this path, including observability log search, is subject to the same refusal. | A test sends DDL, DML, COPY and CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE statements through each caller of the shared SQL path. The callers include log search, and every statement must be refused. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R016` | **UQL AS OF uses Unix-second timestamps, not ISO dates.** UQL's AS OF clause takes a Unix-second timestamp. Query stages use the published UQL stage-separator syntax. They do not use Cypher-shaped text or an ISO-8601 timestamp. Text that does not match this grammar is rejected as invalid rather than silently accepted. A conformance test confirms that UQL text emitted by any client parses successfully against the current grammar. | A parser test feeds the correct stage-chained, Unix-second AS OF form and confirms it parses. A negative test confirms that the parser rejects Cypher-shaped or ISO-timestamp text. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R017` | **Dependency tracking covers edge-type and embedding generations.** Traversal and embedding-ranking plans register their dependencies. They name the edge types and embedding generations they read. A write that changes an unrelated edge type or generation does not invalidate the plan. A write touching a real dependency does. | A test performs disjoint writes against unrelated edge types and embedding generations. It confirms that an existing traversal/rank plan is not invalidated. A dependent write does invalidate it. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R018` | **Class-level volatility drives invalidation and cache freshness.** Classes and sources declare an eg:volatilityClass, and a per-class invalidation event fires when matching data changes. Foreign-source result caching only serves a cached result when the relevant source watermark is current, consistent with the volatility class declared for that data. | A test changes data in a declared volatility class, confirms the corresponding invalidation event fires, and confirms the foreign-source cache only serves fresh, watermark-current results. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R019` | **Per-bar signal-state advancement with revisable trend records.** On each bar finalization, the complex-event-processing layer advances signal state and records a TrendFlip event; a later correction can revise a previously recorded TrendFlip without losing the revision history. A scanner index exposes the latest signal state per series for efficient lookup. | A test finalizes a sequence of bars, confirms signal state advances and TrendFlip records are created, then issues a revision and confirms both the historical and latest-state views are correct. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R020` | **One query-text method surface, with the legacy method retired.** The engine serves exactly one query-text method, Method::Uql. The legacy UnifiedQueryText method and its callers, including remote-engine federation and server tests, are migrated to it. UnifiedQueryText is removed from the codebase with no compatibility alias or deprecation shim. | A contract test confirms UnifiedQueryText no longer exists in the method registry. An integration test confirms remote-engine federation operates correctly over Method::Uql. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R021` | **Full UQL predicate algebra with SQL-consistent evaluation.** UQL's predicate algebra supports the comparison operations, AND/OR/NOT with parentheses, IN, BETWEEN, IS [NOT] NULL, typed literals, JSONPath access and spatial predicates. The per-row three-valued evaluator used by UQL agrees with the equivalent SQL evaluation on every supported predicate, including null handling. | A differential test evaluates the same predicate set through SQL and through the UQL per-row evaluator. It uses null and non-null fixtures and asserts identical results. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R022` | **UQL source clauses are first-class, with typed empty-result cases.** UQL treats MATCH, SPARQL, TSSCAN, SENSOR FUSE/ALIGN, SPATIAL/TENSOR SCAN and FOREIGN SCAN/HTTP as first-class source clauses in its grammar and planner. A head-transform stage that yields an empty result set emits the UQL_W_HEAD_TRANSFORM_YIELDS_EMPTY warning instead of silently reseeding from the runtime, and runtime empty-set reseeding is no longer part of the documented language. | A parser and planner test exercises each source clause and confirms a head-transform-to-empty case emits the warning rather than reseeding. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R022.1` | **A label Scan matches the engine's label set.** The plan executor's label source is `scan_label` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs`. It and the layer filters of the stream, time-series, spatial and tensor sources SHALL select a node by its label. The label can be under `type`, `node_type` or `label`, or in the `labels` array. This is the same set that the engine's label index files (`medulla_core::graph::node_carries_label`). That function shares `NODE_LABEL_KEYS` with `labels_of`. Writers store `node_type`, so a Scan that read only `type` returned no rows for them. | The unit test is `scan_matches_every_label_field_writers_store` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs`. The served test `server::tests::test_served_scan_reads_node_type_labels` returns `node_type` nodes. It uses both `Method::Uql` (`MATCH (:Paper)`) and `Method::UnifiedQuery` (`Op::Scan`). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R023` | **Every query modality has a published UQL spelling.** UQL provides a syntax for every supported query modality: SPARQL/VAR, user-defined functions, FOREIGN SCAN/HTTP with any credential-bearing inline source specification refused, spatial functions including BUFFER, INTERSECTION and DIFFERENCE plus REPROJECT, tensor SLICE/REDUCE/ADD and related tensor functions, complex-event patterns such as SEQ/WITHIN/ABSENCE over SLIDING/TUMBLING windows, SENSOR ALIGN, probabilistic PROB, FUSE K, REASON ONTOLOGY, bounded WINDOW, and VALIDATE SHAPE. | A grammar-exhaustiveness test parses a program covering every listed construct and confirms each round-trips through the canonical printer, and a negative test confirms a credential-bearing FOREIGN SCAN/HTTP specification is refused. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R024` | **Traversal syntax compiles to one canonical Expand encoding.** UQL's directional traversal arrows, the wildcard hop syntax and edge predicate filters all compile to the budget-checked, shortest-hop Op::Expand plan node. The common case of an unfiltered, unbounded, outbound single-relationship expansion is always encoded the same way, spelled Traverse, so the planner has one canonical representation rather than multiple equivalent encodings. | A planner test compiles equivalent traversal syntaxes and confirms they produce the identical Op::Expand/Traverse plan node. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R025` | **Typed parameter binding end-to-end through the Python client.** UQL's typed $param placeholders bind only in parser-recognized value positions and are type-checked. Unbound, mistyped or unused parameters fail as typed errors. The engine serves this through Method::Uql with text and params fields. The generated Python client exposes a matching uql(text, params) call; values never pass through text interpolation. | A test sends injection-shaped parameter values through the Python client and confirms they bind as typed values rather than altering the executed query, plus negative tests for unbound, mistyped and unused parameters. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R026` | **UQL documentation is test-verified with an embedded grammar.** docs/uql.md documents the current UQL language. Every example in a uql code block parses under a dedicated test, and the page embeds the generated grammar rather than a hand-maintained copy. docs/analytics_in_uql.md clearly separates which analytics run through SQL from which run through UQL stages. | A documentation test parses every uql-tagged code block in docs/uql.md. It fails the build when an example does not parse. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R027` | **EXPLAIN, PROFILE and RETURN support multi-stage UQL programs.** A UQL program that composes named LET/JOIN subplans into a dependency graph supports EXPLAIN and PROFILE output and RETURN channels the same as a single-stage query, rather than refusing with UQL_UNSUPPORTED. | An integration test runs EXPLAIN and PROFILE over a multi-stage LET/JOIN program and confirms per-stage plan and runtime information is returned instead of a refusal. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R028` | **`KnowledgeSet` is reachable from UQL.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql` `WITH KNOWLEDGE`. Caller: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`src/server/handlers/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `crates/medulla-plan/src/uql/serve/annotate.rs` → `KnowledgeSet::from_rowset` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/knowledge.rs`). A UQL query reads a `KnowledgeSet` combining epistemic status, `FUSE` and `AS OF` in one statement. The result equals the structured `Method::KnowledgeSet` result and the prior separate surfaces for the same inputs, field by field. Refusals follow R028.1. Writes: none. Depends on: R028.1, R045. | Test: `tests/query/test_knowledge_set_uql_served.py::test_uql_equals_structured_and_prior_surfaces` (L4). It runs equivalent epistemic, fuse and as-of queries on the three surfaces and asserts equal rows and equal knowledge frames. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R029` | **UQL text from graph-exploration clients matches the grammar.** Any UQL text generated by a graph-exploration client, including a temporal scrubber control, uses the stage-chained syntax and a Unix-second AS OF timestamp defined by the published UQL grammar, not Cypher-shaped text or an ISO-8601 timestamp. Text that does not conform to this grammar is rejected with a typed parse error rather than silently misinterpreted. | A conformance test parses the exact text emitted by the temporal-scrubber control's query template and confirms it succeeds under the current grammar. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R030` | **TSSCAN preserves per-series row identity and full precision.** The TSSCAN source identifies each time-series row by `series@ts`. It carries its values as `f64` channels named `v0..vk`. Rows from different series at the same timestamp never collide. No value is truncated to `f32`. This row identity is the basis for series analytics expressed in UQL. `MEDULLA-FINANCE-PRIMITIVES-R002.2.3.15` consumes the `series@ts` identity for `EVALUATE`. | A fixture test scans overlapping multi-series data through TSSCAN. It confirms no row collisions, no truncation and correct per-series, per-timestamp identity. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R031` | **Contribution attribution kernels exposed through UQL ATTRIBUTE.** The engine computes contribution attribution using linear split, exact Shapley for small coalitions, seeded sampled Shapley with a confidence interval, Owen values over a hierarchy, and multi-factor OLS regression with heteroskedasticity-and-autocorrelation-consistent standard errors. UQL exposes these through an ATTRIBUTE channel VALUE agg SHAPLEY [SAMPLES n SEED s] stage. A decision-evaluation report can attribute an assembly slate's outcome across its logged sub-slates. | Exact Shapley results are checked against a brute-force oracle, sampled Shapley against seeded-repeatability and confidence-interval-coverage tests, and the ATTRIBUTE UQL stage against an integration test. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R032` | **Probabilistic impact propagation with cone-scoped recomputation.** Stage: query execution, served `Uql` `PROPAGATE <model> <edge-pattern> HOPS n`, and the standing-query job. Caller: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`src/server/handlers/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/propagate.rs::propagate`; mining entry `src/server/handlers/mining/impact.rs` writes `:ImpactAssessment`. Models: `noisy_or` is exact on acyclic structure, `P(v) = 1 - product over parents u of (1 - p(u) * t(u,v))`, with `t` the ontology-declared edge transmission. Cycles unroll to depth `propagate_unroll_depth` (default 3, `[1, 8]`). `independent_cascade` runs `propagate_samples` seeded Monte Carlo runs (default 2000, `[100, 100000]`) and reports a 95% confidence interval. The seed comes from the request, else the first 8 bytes of the request digest. The result lists the top 5 contributing paths with per-seed attribution. A standing query seeded from `Incident`, `ConformanceViolation` or `CVE` facts recomputes only the affected cone. It writes `ImpactAssessment` facts through the mining writeback, a WAL-replayed mutation. Both knobs are config keys in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`). Refusals: `PROPAGATE_MODEL_UNKNOWN`, `PROPAGATE_HOPS_EXCEEDED`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Depends on: R032.100, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179. | Test: `tests/query/test_propagate_served.py::test_noisy_or_matches_enumeration_and_cascade_is_repeatable` (L4). It compares `noisy_or` with exact enumeration on small acyclic graphs and checks seeded repeatability and the interval for the cascade. `test_standing_query_recomputes_only_cone` asserts a changed seed touches only its cone. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R033` | **Barrier-hit and time-to-exhaustion derivations, one shared CDF.** The engine computes first-passage probability for a barrier using the arithmetic-Brownian-motion inverse-Gaussian form and the geometric-Brownian-motion log-barrier form, each checked against a terminal-distribution calculation, with maximum-likelihood drift and volatility estimation and a bootstrap confidence interval. These are exposed as DERIVE barrier_hit(...) and DERIVE tte(...), and all normal-tail calculations use one public stats::norm_cdf implementation rather than a separate private copy. | Results are checked against a Monte Carlo simulation and against the analytic limit as volatility approaches zero, which must match the deterministic linear-prediction case. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R034` | **Motif and discord search over time series, batch and streaming.** The engine finds motifs and discords in time series using an FFT-accelerated MASS distance computation and a matrix profile, computed anytime for batch data and incrementally for streaming data. UQL exposes MOTIF LIKE $shape LENGTH m TOP k and DISCORD LENGTH m TOP k under the standard query budgets, anomaly mining supports a matrix-profile discord algorithm, and a series shape predicate can emit a stream event consumed by the existing complex-event-processing matcher. | A test compares matrix-profile motif and discord results against a brute-force distance computation on a reference series and confirms the streaming and batch computations agree. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R035` | **Filtered text search evaluates within the filtered candidate set.** A text search combined with a filter evaluates its relevance ranking only within the set of rows that satisfy the filter, using a filtered index scan or equivalent pre-filtering, so a filtered query returns up to k relevant results drawn from the full matching set rather than filtering after an unfiltered top-k ranking has already discarded relevant rows. | A correctness oracle compares filtered BM25 search results against an unfiltered full scan intersected with the filter and confirms they agree. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R036` | **Bounded, observable graph reopen after a restart.** Stage: server bootstrap and read admission. Caller: server bootstrap → `src/server/dispatch/graph_pipeline/graph_access.rs` read gate (`PARTIAL_MATERIALIZATION` at line 335) → `ReopenState` (new, `crates/medulla-core/src/index/reopen.rs`). After restart the engine serves reads from stored data. It restores index state from persisted index state, else rebuilds it in a background task. `ReopenState` is `Cold`, `Materializing{done, total}` or `Ready`. While not `Ready`, reads that need the index return `PARTIAL_MATERIALIZATION` with `done` and `total`; reads of stored rows still succeed. The readiness signal is the existing index manifest report (`crates/medulla-core/src/index.rs`, health call) plus field `ready` in `Health`. Bound: reopen time to first served read is at most 10% of full rebuild time at 100000 documents. Knob: config `reopen_background_threads` in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`), default 2, `[1, 16]`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none to graph data. Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL. | Test: `tests/restart_reopen_partial_materialization.rs::reads_refused_until_ready` (L3). It restarts at 1000, 10000 and 100000 documents. It asserts `PARTIAL_MATERIALIZATION` until `ready`. It asserts the 10% reopen bound. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R037` | **Rolling window statistics are both O(1) and numerically exact.** Streaming rolling-window statistics, such as standard deviation and z-score, update in constant time per step and report exactly zero for a window containing constant values, with no floating-point residue accumulating from incremental add/remove updates over long-running windows. | A property test compares the streaming O(1) update against an exact from-scratch recomputation across constant, alternating and large windows and confirms they agree, including an exact zero on constant windows. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R038` | **Federation optimizer: cost-aware pushdown, freshness-aware cache (rollup).** The query planner models each foreign source's capability. Those capabilities cover SQL/OBDA, SPARQL endpoints, HTTP/REST connectors, lake formats and other registered engine kinds. It pushes down projection, filters, LIMIT/ORDER, aggregates, same-source joins and batched key lookups only when that capability proves the work sound. It applies cardinality and cost estimates under a per-query network budget, and it bounds remote parallelism. It serves freshness-aware cached results gated by source watermarks. EXPLAIN output shows exactly which fragments ran remotely. The child requirements below define the required behavior and acceptance. | A differential test compares optimized execution plans and results against a full-fetch oracle. It covers each supported source capability and asserts correctness, declared pushdown and accurate EXPLAIN reporting. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R038.1` | **Typed foreign-source capability + cost model proves which pushdown kinds are sound.** A `ForeignSourceCapability`/cost-model typed struct declares, per source, a cardinality estimate. It also declares a network-budget cost. It lists pushdown kinds that the source proves sound: projection, filter, limit/order, aggregate, same-source join and batched key lookup. The optimizer consults this model before pushing any fragment down. It refuses a pushdown kind that the capability does not prove sound. | A unit/property test suite validates the typed model's construction and asserts a representative refusal case per pushdown kind: a source whose declared capability does not prove a kind sound is never offered that pushdown. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039` | **Mixed traversal and vector queries return `k` or a typed shortfall.** Stage: query execution, served `UnifiedQuery`. Caller: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `run_unified_with` (`src/server/handlers/query/planning.rs:436`) → `medulla_plan::execute` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs:536`) → `rank_limit` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/vector_rank.rs:56`) → `replenish_until_k` (new, same file). The planner asks the index for `k * oversample` candidates and applies the traversal filter. It asks again with a doubled window until `k` candidates survive or `max_rounds` is reached. Result type `Shortfall{requested, found, reason}` in `crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval.rs`; `reason` is `eligible_exhausted`, `missing_embedding` or `budget_exhausted`. The planner never returns fewer than `k` rows without a `Shortfall`. Config `retrieval_replenish_max_rounds` (default 6, `[1, 32]`) and `retrieval_oversample_factor` (default 4, `[1, 16]`) in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`). Refusal code on a bad request: `RETRIEVAL_K_INVALID` for `k = 0`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends on: R040.1.2, R040.1.4. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_mixed_traversal_topk_served.py::test_k_or_typed_shortfall` (L4). The oracle fixture has two-hop traversal, parallel edges with stable edge ids, and ten permitted matches below the unfiltered top-k. It asserts exactly `k` rows or a `Shortfall` naming the found count. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040` | **Filter-aware index scans for filtered vector and text search.** Stage: query execution, served `UnifiedQuery`. Caller: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `run_unified_with` (`src/server/handlers/query/planning.rs:436`) → `medulla_plan::execute` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs:536`). Then `rank_limit` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/vector_rank.rs:56`) → `HnswIndex::search_filtered` (`crates/medulla-ann`) and the `BM25` scan with a bitmap filter. The planner passes the pre-filtered set or bitmap to the index. It never scans the full index and discards rows afterward. Targets on the fixed corpus of `crates/medulla-ann/benches/filtered_ann.rs`: filtered p50 latency at most 1.5 times the unfiltered p50 at 1% selectivity. Recall equals the post-filter brute-force oracle. The benchmark never gates a PR. Refusal: `FILTER_UNEVALUABLE` when the filter cannot compile to a bitmap. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends on: R040.1. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_filtered_scan_benchmark_served.py::test_filtered_scan_matches_oracle` (L4). | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R041` | **Federation optimizer core drives pushdown decisions for every query.** The federation optimizer in medulla-plan/src/federation_opt/ models each foreign source's capabilities, builds a RemoteRequest per source, tracks a per-query budget meter, trace and LIMIT hints, maintains learned SourceStats with provenance, and executes bind joins with an adaptive batch sizer and typed refusals; it is bound into the ForeignScan/Foreign plan arms so every served plan uses it. | Federation optimizer unit and integration tests exercise capability-gated pushdown, budget enforcement, bind-join batching and typed refusal paths against the served query path. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R042` | **OBDA pushes per-predicate scans and semi-join reduction to SQL sources.** OBDA execution groups scans per predicate, pushes constant-subject key lookups and constant-object equality checks to the external SQL source, and reduces semi-joins to an IN clause rendered as ObdaCompare::In by that source. | OBDA pushdown tests (FQR-06) confirm per-predicate scan grouping, constant lookup/equality pushdown and semi-join IN reduction produce results matching the full-fetch oracle. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R043` | **SQL foreign-source connections share the one outbound SSRF guard.** SQL DSN connections for Sql foreign-source specs and OBDA external sources are validated at a single chokepoint against the same outbound allowlist and sensitive-address-range checks used for HTTP foreign sources. | Security negative tests confirm a disallowed SQL DSN target is refused with the same stable typed error as a disallowed HTTP target. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R044` | **UQL EXPLAIN and PROFILE surface federation trace and budget hints.** UQL's PROFILE and EXPLAIN output include a federation trace describing remote fragments, residual local work and budget usage, and callers can supply budget hints that the planner honors when routing a query through the remote UQL method. | EXPLAIN/PROFILE tests for federated UQL queries confirm the trace reports the remote/residual breakdown and that caller-supplied budget hints are respected. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R045` | **Foreign rows carry typed columns for filter and projection pushdown.** A foreign source returns ForeignRows with stable column names, types, null semantics and row provenance, and apply_filter/apply_projection classify each operation as Exact, Inexact or Unsupported; only an Exact result removes the local residual, an Inexact result may narrow transfer only to a superset of authorized matching rows, and an Unsupported result fetches the needed columns and evaluates locally without ever dropping a column required by a residual, ORDER BY, authorization or proof channel. | A recording mock verifies every pushed ForeignRows request narrows data transfer without changing answers, and a schema/version mismatch or ambiguous column identity is refused rather than silently bound. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R046` | **`OBDA` pushes `SPARQL` `LIMIT`, `ORDER BY`, aggregates and same-source joins.** Stage: query execution, served `Sparql` over an `OBDA` source. Caller: served `Sparql` → `src/server/sparql_http.rs` handler → `crates/medulla-rdf/src/obda.rs` planner → `obda/direct.rs` direct evaluation. The translator renders `LIMIT`, `ORDER BY`, aggregate and same-source join clauses into SQL for the external source. It evaluates the pushed solutions directly. It builds no transient in-memory graph for the pushed part. A clause the source does not declare as pushable (R041 capability set) stays local. Refusal: `OBDA_PUSHDOWN_UNSUPPORTED` only when the caller forces pushdown. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none; the query path is read-only. Depends on: R041, R042. | Test: `tests/query/test_obda_pushdown_served.py::test_pushed_results_equal_full_fetch` (L4). It compares pushed results with the full-fetch oracle. It asserts no transient graph exists for the pushed part. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R047` | **Federation enforces per-source parallelism caps and rate limits.** Each foreign source is bounded by a global semaphore keyed to its fingerprint that caps concurrent requests and enforces a rate limit, while bind-join batches to different sources still execute in parallel within those caps. | Concurrency tests confirm request counts to a single source never exceed its configured cap while independent sources execute concurrently. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R048` | **Join ordering across foreign sources uses learned, provenanced statistics.** The planner orders joins across multiple foreign sources using learned cardinality statistics persisted as graph facts with their provenance, seeded by registration-time probes such as PostgreSQL table-size statistics, engine-reported statistics and VoID descriptions. (Rollup: delivered incrementally by its `.1`, `.2`, ... children, one per code root — typed model, persistence, per-source-kind probes, planner join-ordering use.) | Join-ordering tests compare planner choices against known cardinalities and confirm learned statistics carry sample count and provenance per FQR-05. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R048.1` | **Typed join-cardinality-statistic model with provenance.** `crates/medulla-plan/src/federation_stats.rs` defines `JoinCardinalityStatistic` (estimated cardinality, sample count, a source generation marker) and `StatisticsProvenance` (PostgreSQL table-size stats, engine-reported stats, or a VoID description), constructed only through a fallible constructor that refuses a statistic carrying no provenance. This is the typed-model code root of R048; persistence as graph facts, per-source-kind probes and planner join-ordering use are later `.2`+ children. | A unit test constructs a statistic with each provenance kind and confirms it round-trips its sample count and provenance, and a second test confirms the constructor refuses a statistic given no provenance. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R049` | **Foreign query fragment cache is freshness-aware and owner-salted.** Cached results for a foreign query fragment are keyed by source identity, an owner-specific salt, the canonical request and the source's current freshness watermark; a missing or stale watermark disables the cache hit rather than serving stale data. | Cache tests confirm a hit requires a matching fresh watermark and that a stale or absent watermark forces a live fetch. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R050` | **SPARQL SERVICE uses bind joins and pushes FILTER/LIMIT.** SPARQL SERVICE clauses execute as VALUES-based bind joins against the remote endpoint and push FILTER and LIMIT into the SERVICE request where sound, validated through the same single outbound SSRF guard used by other foreign sources. | SERVICE bind-join tests confirm multivalued RDF answers are preserved and that a disallowed SERVICE endpoint is refused by the shared SSRF guard. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R051` | **Iceberg scans prune manifests using the final pushed predicates.** The Iceberg table scan is assembled only after the query engine has supplied its filters, projection and limit, so manifest pruning reflects the actual pushed predicates instead of an earlier, less-informed scan; documentation describing pruning behavior is corrected to match this. | A partitioned Iceberg fixture test confirms manifest files are pruned according to the final pushed filters, projection and limit. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R052` | **RemoteEngine sources receive pushed key filters and bounded LIMIT.** For registered RemoteEngine sources, the planner appends authorized key filters and a bounded LIMIT to the canonical read-only UQL request, preserving ORDER BY, residual filtering, pagination and outer-query limit semantics; a remote lacking the feature or exact push capability falls back to the safe bounded route with local residuals instead of fetching an unbounded result. | A recording mock proves pushed RemoteEngine requests narrow transfer without changing answers, and a capability-lacking remote is verified to fall back safely. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R053` | **`Trino`, `Cypher` and `Spark` sources are typed `ForeignSourceSpec` kinds.** Stage: federation planning, served `Uql` `FOREIGN` and `UnifiedQuery`. Caller: served `Uql` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_uql` (`src/server/handlers/query/uql_statement.rs:17`) → `crates/medulla-plan/src/federation_capabilities.rs` capability lookup → `federation_trino.rs`, `federation_cypher_unbound.rs`, `federation_spark.rs`. Each kind has one capability set. `Trino`: filter, projection, limit, order, aggregate, same-source join. `Cypher`: filter, projection, limit, order. `Spark` (`SparkBatch`): batch read of a sealed artifact only, no pushdown. A planner never pushes an operation outside the kind's set. Refusal: `FOREIGN_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none; the query path is read-only. Depends on: R041, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R024.1. | Test: `crates/medulla-plan/src/federation_capabilities.rs::tests::kind_advertises_only_declared_pushdowns` (L2). Verified by the served test `tests/query/test_foreign_kinds_served.py::test_capability_matrix` (L4). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R054` | **`SPARQL` `SERVICE` and peer federation share the one outbound `SSRF` guard.** Stage: outbound request admission. Both call `validate_outbound_http_target` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/federation_ssrf.rs:55`) with `OutboundAllowPolicy`. No second validator exists in either path. Refusal: `OUTBOUND_TARGET_DENIED`, identical from both paths. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends on: R043. | Test: `tests/query/test_outbound_guard_served.py::test_denied_target_refused_identically` (L4). It sends one disallowed target through `SERVICE` and through peer fan-out and asserts the same code. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R055` | **SPARQL evaluation binds every value of a multi-valued property.** The SPARQL evaluator iterates and binds every value of a repeated predicate on a subject, rather than binding only the first value and silently dropping the remaining matching answers. | A regression test on a graph with two values for the same predicate confirms both values bind, including per-binding FILTER evaluation, per FQR-01's correct-read acceptance. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R056` | **Causal-impact p-values reuse the shared numeric tail functions.** medulla-compute/src/mining/causal_impact.rs computes its p-value using medulla-numeric's shared normal survival and cumulative distribution functions instead of a local error-function approximation, avoiding the catastrophic cancellation that approximation produced in the tail. | A tail-probability test against literal reference values at tight relative tolerance confirms the computed p-value matches the exact reference in the tail, per FQR-09. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R057` | **SQL provider module stays within the repository's file-size limit.** crates/medulla-query/src/sql/providers.rs is kept at or under the repository's 900-line file-size limit through semantic extraction that preserves existing provider behavior exactly. | A line-count check confirms the file is at or under 900 lines, and the existing SQL provider test suite passes unchanged after extraction. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058` | **Natural language runs only as disclosed, read-only typed UQL.** Stage: query planning, served `NlQuery`, `NlQueryExplain`, `NlQueryPlan`. Caller: served `NlQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` → `handle_nl_query_plan` (`src/server/handlers/query/nl.rs:58`) → `NlPlanner` port → UQL parse → the read-only pipeline of `Uql`. The engine parses planner output with the grammar every caller uses. It runs under the caller's verified authority, row visibility and budgets. Each result carries the executed UQL. Plan-only mode returns the typed UQL candidate and reads no data. Results are never cached on the natural-language text. No mutating or side-effecting operation is reachable. Rollup over R058.1 (disclosure model), R058.2 (dispatch and result contract), R058.3 (no-cache and no-mutation guards), R058.4 (planning context). Refusals: `NL_PLAN_UNPARSEABLE`, `NL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, `NL_MUTATION_REFUSED`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends on: MEDULLA-CQ-CONN. | The root journey test is `tests/nl_query_served.rs::executed_uql_returned_and_read_only` (L3). It asserts executed UQL, plan-only without reads, each refusal, direct-UQL row parity and no reachable mutation. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.1` | **Typed NL→UQL disclosure model.** `medulla-plan` exposes an `NlQueryResult` that always carries the exact UQL text alongside any data — `Some(rows)` when executed, `None` (plan-only mode) when not — plus a typed `NlQueryError` with dedicated variants for planner output that fails to parse and output that exceeds a configured `NlQueryBudget`, refused before parsing or execution. `plan_only` produces the candidate without taking any view/`PlanCtx`, so plan-only mode cannot read data even by construction. | Unit tests confirm: an executed result carries the exact UQL that ran; `plan_only` returns a candidate with `rows: None` and reads no data; malformed planner output is refused with `NlQueryError::ParseFailed` (not executed); and over-budget planner output is refused with `NlQueryError::BudgetExceeded` before parse/execute, in both plan-only and executing modes. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2` | **Dispatch handler and result contract disclose the typed NL→UQL model.** Child slice of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058` (see tasks.md). Rollup over `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.1` (dispatch-path budget enforcement against the typed model, built) and `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.2` (serve the executed UQL in the result contract and expose a plan-only entry point to callers). | Tests confirm the executed UQL is returned with the result, plan-only mode returns a candidate without reading data, planner output that fails to parse or exceeds a budget is refused with a typed error, row visibility matches the equivalent direct UQL query, and no mutation is reachable |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.1` | **Dispatch handler enforces the typed NL budget before parsing.** `Method::NlQuery`'s dispatch handler (`handle_nl_query_plan`) applies the SAME character-count `NlQueryBudget` the typed disclosure model (`MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.1`) defines to the planner's produced UQL text BEFORE it reaches `medulla_plan::uql::parse`, refusing an over-budget answer with the typed `UQL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` code rather than running unbounded planner text into the parser. | A unit test confirms `NlQueryBudget::check` refuses over-budget text with a typed `BudgetExceeded` error and accepts in-budget text, independent of whether that text later parses. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.2` | **Served NL result carries the executed UQL; plan-only is reachable.** This row holds the remaining scope of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2`. A served method returns the executed UQL text with the rows. A caller can also reach the typed model's `plan_only`. Other clients depend on the `Method::NlQuery` result, so it stays unchanged. A sibling method carries the disclosure. Rollup over `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.2.1` (served `NlQueryExplain`, built) and `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.2.2` (plan-only entry point). | See each child. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.2.1` | **Served `NlQueryExplain` returns the executed UQL with the rows.** `Method::NlQueryExplain { text, graph }` takes the same request as `Method::NlQuery`. It routes the same way and has the same `query:nl` read policy. An empty `graph` falls back to the request envelope's graph. Both handlers share one run, `run_nl_query` in `src/server/handlers/query/nl.rs`. There, `handle_nl_query_plan` returns the planner's exact UQL text with the parsed plan. The same legs, row-level-security snapshot and off-lock run then produce the rows. The engine answers with `medulla_types::nl_query::NlQueryExplainResult`: `{"uql": <UQL text>, "rows": [[node_id, score or null], ...]}`. The engine never caches this result. The `NlQuery` result stays byte-identical. The Python client exposes `QueryClient.nl_query_explain(text, graph=None)`. | Served tests confirm that `uql` equals the planner's output. They confirm that `rows` equal the `NlQuery` rows for the same text. A parse failure (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`) refuses with the same code and detail as `NlQuery`. An over-budget answer (`UQL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`) does too. A wire test confirms the Python call shape. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.2.2.2` | **Plan-only NL entry point.** Expose the typed model's `plan_only` as a served read that returns the candidate UQL without reading data. | A test confirms a plan-only call returns the candidate and reads no data. Not yet delivered. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.3` | **No-cache-on-NL-text and no-mutation-reachable guarantees.** Child slice of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058` (see tasks.md). Rollup over `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.3.1` (no-cache-on-NL-text guard, built) and `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.3.2` (no-mutation-reachable confirmation). | Tests confirm the executed UQL is returned with the result, plan-only mode returns a candidate without reading data, planner output that fails to parse or exceeds a budget is refused with a typed error, row visibility matches the equivalent direct UQL query, and no mutation is reachable |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.3.1` | **NL results are never cached on the NL text.** `Method::NlQuery`'s handler answers through the uncached `result_response` path and never references the engine's `ResultCache`, so a non-deterministic planner answer can never be served stale from a prior, differently-planned request; a regression guard pins this structural guarantee. | A test confirms the handler source never references `ResultCache` or the cache-aware `served_response` helper. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.3.2` | **No mutating operation is reachable from the NL surface.** Remaining scope of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R058.3`: confirm no NL-planned UQL text can reach a mutating or side-effecting operation. | A negative test confirms an NL-planned mutating statement is refused. Not yet delivered. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R059` | **Semantic index lifecycle is fenced, durable and restart-safe.** Semantic and vector index creation, backfill, activation and drop are fenced, durable operations, and an unavailable index generation falls back correctly rather than serving incorrect or partial results after restart. | Restart and fenced-lifecycle tests (FQR-07) confirm index generations activate, drop and recover correctly across a restart. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R060` | **Leiden community detection supports a Constant Potts Model quality function.** LeidenConfig exposes a Constant Potts Model quality function as an alternative to modularity, applying its own delta-quality term inside the existing local-moving and refinement loop, so community detection is not limited to modularity's resolution limit. | Leiden kernel tests and the corresponding query-surface objective-function parameter confirm the Constant Potts Model produces correct communities distinct from the modularity result on the same graph. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R061` | **One grammar table drives UQL docs, prompts and error messages.** The UQL grammar table is the single source for the generated EBNF documentation block, the natural-language planner's prompt, and the expected-token lists in parser error messages; the dispatch table is checked against the grammar in both directions, and a feature unsupported in the current build fails with one dedicated feature-not-available error. | A generated-docs snapshot test and a dispatch-table-versus-grammar consistency check (FQR-01) both pass, and a feature excluded from compilation is confirmed to fail with the dedicated error. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R062` | **Streaming memory test measures only its own allocations.** The million-row streaming-scan memory test measures memory allocated by the stream under test using a scoped counting allocator, rather than process-wide memory, so the test cannot fail due to unrelated concurrent work in the same process or host. | The test passes measuring peak allocation for the streamed scan below its declared bound when run both in isolation and alongside concurrent unrelated tests. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R063` | **Leiden provides stable community naming and a connectivity check.** Community detection runs a sweep across multiple resolution values and keeps communities that persist across them, names each stable community by its highest-centrality member rather than by an index that can shuffle between runs, and performs a connectivity post-check on every detected community. | Tests cover the resolution sweep's stability selection, confirm community names stay stable across reruns on the same input, and confirm the connectivity post-check detects any disconnected community. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R064` | **UQL lexer supports its full token set and structured diagnostics.** The UQL lexer tokenizes comments, scientific notation, Unicode and quoted identifiers, UTF-8 strings, parameter placeholders and JSONPath tokens, enforces bounded nesting, and reports structured diagnostics with an error code, source span, expected-token set, fix hint and a line/column caret. | Lexer tests cover each token class and confirm diagnostic output includes the code, span, expected set and caret position for malformed input. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R065` | **Named UQL sub-plans compile to an ordered plan DAG.** Named sub-plans referenced by FROM or JOIN compile into a plan DAG ordered by first-reference depth-first traversal, a FUSE operation inlines named sub-plans, and the DAG printer's output can be re-parsed back to an equivalent plan. EXPLAIN and PROFILE are served for a DAG-shaped program, node by node and truthfully: the program is reported as written with one stage per node and its estimate, PROFILE records each node's rows and time, and the result states that such a program is not cost-reordered and not incrementally maintained. | Tests confirm depth-first compile ordering, FUSE inlining correctness via re-parse, and that EXPLAIN and PROFILE on a DAG program return one stage per node with the not-reordered, not-incremental note. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R066` | **SPARQL evaluator is split into modules within the file-size limit.** crates/medulla-rdf/src/sparql.rs is split into cohesive modules so that no resulting file exceeds the repository's 900-line limit, using pure code moves that preserve every existing function and behavior unchanged. | The full medulla-rdf all-features test suite passes identically before and after the split, and a function-by-function comparison confirms every original function is preserved. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R067` | **UQL RETURN exposes multiple named score channels per row.** Each scoring stage, such as similarity, belief or window scoring, records its score under its own fixed channel in a side table, so a query can return several distinct named scores for the same row and served results carry every requested channel. | A test returning two or more named score channels in the same query confirms each row carries the correct value for every requested channel. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R068` | **UQL supports WITH PROOF for proof-carrying results.** UQL gains a WITH PROOF clause that returns proof-carrying results consistent with MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R006's proof contract, binding each returned proof to its premises and source generation. | A test issuing a UQL query WITH PROOF confirms the returned proof matches MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R006's premise-binding and source/generation requirements. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R069` | **SQL pushdown batches key lookups and shares rendering with OBDA.** SQL foreign-source pushdown batches key lookups into a single IN-list subquery and pushes LIMIT into the statement's parsed syntax tree while respecting any existing ORDER BY or LIMIT and skipping FETCH or non-literal limits; both SQL pushdown and OBDA render literals and identifiers through the same shared rendering function. | Pushdown tests confirm batched IN-list queries and pushed LIMIT match the full-fetch oracle, and OBDA's rendered SQL uses the identical shared rendering function. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R070` | **UQL execution budgets are enforced and reported in EXPLAIN/PROFILE.** A query's execution is bounded by a budget on maximum result rows and maximum traversal visits, failing with a dedicated budget-exceeded error when exceeded; EXPLAIN reports the canonical and optimized plan with per-stage estimated rows and an incremental-maintainability reason, and PROFILE reports per-stage observed rows and elapsed time. | Tests confirm a query exceeding its budget fails with the dedicated budget-exceeded error, and EXPLAIN/PROFILE output includes the documented per-stage fields. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R071` | **HTTP foreign sources support templated pagination and key placeholders.** An HTTP foreign source's URL query part may declare placeholders for keys, limit, offset and page, which the planner fills with percent-encoded values and paginates up to the source's declared page/limit budget; a source requiring keys refuses a full scan that supplies none. | Tests confirm correct percent-encoded substitution for each placeholder, pagination stopping at the declared budget, and a keys-required source refusing a keyless full-scan request. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R072` | **The ontology selects the sources for a cross-source question.** A caller sends the ontology classes of a question. For each class on the path, Medulla names the approved virtual mapping that serves it. A class with no mapping is named as uncovered. Every selection carries its premises: label, relation and mapping facts with their source generation. The answer is read-only and plans no execution. Children: `.1` and `.2` deliver it, and further children land the live-ontology search. Consumer `.2`: `UnifiedQuery` source planning calls the `SourceCoverage` resolver of `MEDULLA-SRC-AUTH-R004.6.1`, not a private path. | A fixture with two sources proves the path and the selected mappings. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R072.1` | **Typed model for cross-source coverage, proved against a fixture.** `medulla-rdf::source_selection` defines `CoverageResult` (`Covered`/`Uncovered`/`Disconnected`), `SourceSelectionHop`, and `Premise` (label/relation/subclass/mapping/source-generation facts), and a fixture-driven `select_sources` that links classes through object properties within a hop budget, entails asserted `subClassOf` facts, and reports read-only coverage with no execution plan attached. | Unit tests over a two-source fixture prove a direct mapping, a subclass mapping serving its superclass, an uncovered reachable class, and a disconnected class. |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R083.3` | **Wiring.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, arbitration step. Caller: `Method::RetrievalRound` → `run_round` → `arbitrate`. The `arbitration` part carries the `prior` and `retrieved` candidates and the query digest. The harness produces both candidates and sends them. Medulla verifies their citations. `run_round` runs `arbitrate` last and returns the verdict in `RoundResult.arbitration`. A call without an `arbitration` part returns none. No production caller of arbitration exists today. This row creates it. Depends on: R081.11, R083.2. | Test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::arbitration_part_returns_verdict_or_none` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R083.4` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, arbitration step. Register in the family `REFUSALS` table (R010.100). An abstaining verdict returns the code `ARBITRATION_ABSTAIN` with both candidates and no chosen answer. A candidate without `grounding` refuses with `CANDIDATE_GROUNDING_REQUIRED`. A citation that fails verification lowers support and never raises it. Depends on: R083.3. | Test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::abstain_returns_both_candidates` (L3) asserts the code, two candidates and no answer. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R083.5` | **Evidence.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, arbitration step. Caller: `run_round` after `arbitrate`. The step appends a `RetrievalPremise` named `retrieval.arbitration`. It holds both candidates with their `grounding` and `support_state`. It also holds the citation ids, the margin and the choice. The harness persists it through the `DecisionLog` run outcome of MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.102. Depends on: R083.3. | Test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::arbitration_premise_lists_both_origins` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R083.6` | **Hot-path proof.** Entry point: served `Method::RetrievalRound` with an `arbitration` part. Caller chain: wire dispatch → `run_round` → `arbitrate`. The Python test sends candidates whose confidence gap is below the margin and whose citations both verify. It expects `ARBITRATION_ABSTAIN` with both candidates. A second case sends a supported 0.70 candidate against an unsupported 0.95 candidate and expects the supported one. A third case cites a document of another tenant and expects `unknown`. Depends on: R083.3 to R083.5. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_arbitration_served.py::test_small_gap_abstains_with_both` and `test_supported_lower_confidence_beats_unsupported_prior` (L4). |

| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1` | **Shared multimodal space and missing-embedding shortfall (rollup).** Stage: query execution, served `UnifiedQuery`. Root family: retrieval. Children `.1` to `.6` deliver it: model, behavior, wiring, refusal, evidence and the hot-path proof. Production caller of every child: served `UnifiedQuery` → `run_unified_with` → `medulla_plan::execute`. Depends on: R039, R040. | The root journey test is `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_sixteen_modality_pairs` (L4). |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.1` | **Multimodal space: model.** Stage: query execution. Extend `EmbeddingSpaceRef` in `crates/medulla-types/src/embedding.rs:47` with `modalities: Vec<ModalityBinding>`; `Modality` is `text`, `image`, `audio` or `video`. The space identity tuple is `(space_id, dim, model_revision)`. One pinned space holds all four bindings. A space without the identity tuple is invalid. Refusal: `EMBEDDING_SPACE_INVALID`. Writes: none. Depends on: none. | Test: `crates/medulla-types/src/embedding.rs::tests::modality_bindings_round_trip` (L1). It round-trips four bindings and rejects an absent identity. Verified by the root slice test `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_sixteen_modality_pairs`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.2` | **Multimodal space: behavior.** Stage: query execution. Caller: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `run_unified_with` (`src/server/handlers/query/planning.rs:436`) → `medulla_plan::execute` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs:536`) → `pair_allowed` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/vector_rank.rs`). Policy `compatible_space_only`: a query modality ranks against a target modality if and only if both bind the same `(space_id, dim, model_revision)` tuple. All sixteen pairs of four modalities follow this rule. The policy keeps R039 replenishment and R040 hard exclusions. Refusal: `EMBEDDING_SPACE_INCOMPATIBLE`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends on: R039.1.1. | Test: `crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/vector_rank.rs::tests::sixteen_pairs_match_oracle` (L2). It compares each pair with an independent permitted-candidate oracle. Verified by the root slice test `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_sixteen_modality_pairs`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.3` | **Multimodal space: wiring.** Stage: query execution. Caller: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `run_unified_with` (`src/server/handlers/query/planning.rs:436`) → `medulla_plan::execute` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs:536`). Then `run_unified_bind_embedder` (`src/server/handlers/query/planning.rs:623`, called at line 529) → `bind_modality_encoders` (new, same file) → `rank_top` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/vector_rank.rs:65`). Decision: encoders live in an engine-local model registry, one entry per modality, type `ModalityEncoderRegistry` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/embed_registry.rs`. Retrieval stays inside medulla and calls no external service. The bind step looks up the registry entry whose space tuple equals the request space. Refusal: `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. Depends on: R039.1.1. | Test: `tests/retrieval/test_multimodal_served.py::test_registry_encoders_reach_index` (L4). A served `UnifiedQuery` must reach the registry encoder and the maintained index, and it asserts `QUERY_ENCODER_UNAVAILABLE` for a missing entry. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.1.4` | **Multimodal space: refusal.** Stage: query execution. Caller: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `run_unified_with` (`src/server/handlers/query/planning.rs:436`) → `medulla_plan::execute` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs:536`) → `rank_top`. When eligible target items lack embeddings, the planner returns `Shortfall{reason: missing_embedding}` with requested, returned and authorized missing counts, and pads nothing. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. This row adds `HierarchyRetrievalPolicy::validate_sections`, which checks one root, acyclic single-parent links and nested source spans. All community fields stay unchanged. A served request must leave `section_nodes` empty. Section records come from stored `SectionNode` graph nodes. A served request with non-empty `section_nodes` refuses `RETRIEVAL_CONTRACT_INVALID`. | Test: `crates/medulla-types/src/retrieval_verdict.rs::tests::section_tree_validation_rejects_cycles_and_bad_spans` (L1). | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.9.7.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, hierarchy step. `retrieve_vector_free` in `crates/medulla-plan/src/leanrag.rs` traverses breadth first over authorized stored summaries and source spans. It selects records of the requested `SectionGranularity` and returns the root-to-record path for each. Child order is the deterministic tie order. The traversal makes zero model, embedding and vector calls. Branch choice by reasoning is the harness's: it narrows the next round with `expand_ids`. Limits: `knob retrieval.section_max_depth` 32 [1, 64] levels and `knob retrieval.section_max_visited` 4096 [1, 65536] records. The traversal never falls back to flat retrieval. Caller: `Method::RetrievalRound` → `run_round` → `retrieve_vector_free`. `run_round` builds a `SectionTopology` over stored `SectionNode` graph nodes of the caller's row-level-security snapshot in `src/server/handlers/query/hierarchy_source.rs`. Request fields are `root_id`, `document_revision` and `granularity`. The result adds the selected section ids to the evidence set. The call makes no embedding or vector-index access. No production caller supplies this path today. Depends on: R081.11, R081.9.7.1. | Test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::section_traversal_returns_spans_with_paths` (L3) asserts zero embedder and vector-index calls through the span counters. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R081.9.7.4` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: retrieval decisions, hierarchy step. `VectorFreeError` maps to `RETRIEVAL_HIERARCHY_INVALID` for a cycle, dangling parent, stale summary, invalid span, revision mismatch or missing granularity. `TraversalLimitExceeded` maps to `RETRIEVAL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` with the exhausted limit. A partial path carries `incomplete: true` in the receipt. The step stops before it reads past a limit. It never converts to community retrieval. Depends on: R081.9.7.3. | Test: `tests/retrieval_round_served.rs::malformed_trees_and_limits_refuse` (L3) covers each mapping. |

Tolerance for the oracle: absolute `1e-6` per component. Refusal: `NO_EMBEDDING` for empty input or a zero-norm mean. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Decision: `semantic_index/worker.rs` is the single executor of the approved encoding recipe. `MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.2` calls it and keeps no second worker. The worker runs the recipe (R039.2.2 to .4) and publishes the generation only after every chunk validates. Served ingestion and query share one encoding policy and vector generation. Refusal: `ENCODE_POLICY_STALE`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: the new vector generation through the semantic store (`SemanticStore`). | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.2.6` | **Chunk encoding: refusal.** Stage: ingest encoding. Caller: `worker.rs:175`. Refusal codes: `ENCODE_POSITION_MODE_UNSUPPORTED`, `ENCODE_DIM_MISMATCH`, `ENCODE_NON_FINITE`, `ENCODE_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`, `ENCODE_POLICY_STALE`. Knob: config `encode_budget_chunks` in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`), default 4096 per document, `[1, 100000]`. A failed chunk blocks the aggregate and leaves the prior generation intact. Empty input and zero-norm aggregates return `NO_EMBEDDING`. The filtered index consumes only complete aggregates and rejects a stale policy generation before ranking. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.6.1` | **Column advice: model.** Stage: SQL read. Caller: served `Sql` → `dispatch_query_family` → `handle_sql_read` (`src/server/handlers/query/sql_read.rs:100`) → `ColumnVectorAdvice` beside `EmbeddingBinding` (`crates/medulla-query/src/tables/embedding_binding.rs:398`). Fields: `table`, `column`, `schema_revision`, `window_start`, `window_end`, `sample_count`, `eligibility` (`eligible`, `unsupported_type`, `insufficient_observations`), `reason`, `score`. Advice is read-only. Writes: none. Depends on: none. | Test: `crates/medulla-query/src/tables/embedding_binding.rs::tests::advice_round_trip` (L1). It round-trips ranked advice and `unsupported_type` without creating a binding. Verified by the root slice test `tests/retrieval/test_column_advice_served.py::test_workload_changes_advice`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.6.2` | **Column advice: workload-first behavior.** Stage: SQL read. Caller: served `Sql` → `dispatch_query_family` → `handle_sql_read` (`src/server/handlers/query/sql_read.rs:100`) → `rank_columns` (new, `crates/medulla-query/src/tables/column_advice.rs`). Policy `workload_first`. Type admission runs first. Score = `0.6 * demand + 0.25 * norm_avg_length + 0.15 * distinct_ratio`. `demand` is the column's share of text-search observations in the window, `norm_avg_length` is average length over 512 capped at 1. Ties break by column id. Fewer than `column_advice_min_samples` observations (config in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`), default 50, `[1, 100000]`) yields `insufficient_observations`. Weights are knobs `knob sql_advice.w_demand`, `.w_length`, `.w_distinct` with defaults 0.6, 0.25, 0.15, `[0, 1]`, unit weight (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179). Writes: none. Depends on: R077.6.3. | Test: `crates/medulla-query/src/tables/column_advice.rs::tests::traffic_changes_rank_not_statistics` (L2). Verified by the root slice test `tests/retrieval/test_column_advice_served.py::test_workload_changes_advice`. |

The advice reads through the authorized catalog relation `information_schema.vector_advice` (new virtual relation, bounded to 1000 rows). A bounded counter table holds 100000 columns. The window is `column_advice_window_hours`, default 24, `[1, 720]`, config in `SchedulerConfig` (`crates/medulla-types/src/scheduler_config.rs`). Writes: counters only; no binding, no queue. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R040.2.2` | **Topic fusion: behavior.** Stage: query execution. Caller: served `UnifiedQuery` → `dispatch_query_family` (`src/server/handlers/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `run_unified_with` (`src/server/handlers/query/planning.rs:436`) → `medulla_plan::execute` (`crates/medulla-plan/src/exec.rs:536`) → `fuse_topic_dense` (new, `crates/medulla-plan/src/exec/topic.rs`). Policy `weighted_topic_dense`: `score = (1 - w) * dense + w * topic`, `w` in `[0, 1]`. `dense` is the min-max normalized similarity over the candidate set. `topic` is the sum over topics of responsibility times cosine similarity to the centroid. Ties break by candidate id ascending. Hard constraints run before topic scoring. Knob: `knob retrieval.topic_weight` default 0.3, `[0, 1]`, unit ratio. Refusal: `TOPIC_WEIGHT_INVALID` for a non-finite or out-of-range `w`. Codes register in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`. Writes: none. | `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.1` | **Model.** Subsystem: RDF validation. `Method::ShexValidate` is declared in `crates/medulla-types/src/protocol/method/method_07.rs`. `ShexValidationReport` is in `crates/medulla-types/src/rdf_report.rs`. The request carries ShExJ schema text, optional Turtle data and ordered node-IRI and shape-label pairs. The report keeps result order, node lexical form, shape, `conforms` and an optional reason. An empty shape map gives zero results and a true aggregate. It is never a whole-graph validation claim. No version suffix exists on these types. | Test: `tests/shex_served.rs::wire_report_preserves_shape_map` (L3) sends a mixed map through dispatch and asserts order and the empty-map result. |

Empty or whitespace-only data_graph exports the authority-projected request graph. The operation reads these inputs without importing the inline graph or persisting a schema. Do not infer a broader durable snapshot from this exported core view. | Planned: tests/shex_served.rs::inline_and_live_data_selection. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.3` | **Behavior: supported constraints.** Subsystem: RDF validation. Caller chain: wire dispatch → `try_handle_shex_validation` in `src/server/handlers/rdf/dispatch.rs` → `handle_shex_validate` in `src/server/handlers/rdf/validation.rs` → `medulla_shex::validate`. The matcher in `crates/medulla-shex/src/validate.rs` checks node kind, datatype, value sets, string facets and numeric facets. Triple constraints count matching outgoing arcs against minimum and maximum bounds. `EachOf` consumes matches in sequence. Closed shapes and extra predicates use the current matcher. No new matcher behavior is added. | Test: `tests/shex_served.rs::bounded_constraints_report_results` (L3) serves one conforming and one nonconforming fixture and asserts a specific reason for the second. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.4` | **RETIRED: known limits are documentation, not a requirement.** The journey test of `R084.8` keeps the matcher limits visible. It asserts first-branch `OneOf` and greedy shared predicates. It asserts the ignored inverse flag and the depth-40 assumption. | Retired; see `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.8`. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.5` | **Wiring.** Subsystem: RDF validation. Caller chain: wire dispatch → `try_handle_shex_validation` in `src/server/handlers/rdf/dispatch.rs` → `handle_shex_validate` in `src/server/handlers/rdf/validation.rs` → `medulla_shex::validate`. The handler calls `Schema::from_shexj`, `ShapeMap::from_iri_pairs`, `validate` and `shex_report_wire`. The `shex` feature supplies the handler. The method stays classified as an internal read. No new parser, planner path or wire method is added. | Test: `tests/shex_served.rs::real_dispatch_reaches_shex_validator` (L3) asserts the response comes from the validator. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.6` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: RDF validation. `handle_shex_validate` keeps three distinct failures: a bad schema, bad inline data and a failed live export. Each returns a typed error with its own message prefix. An unknown shape label returns a nonconforming result with a reason, not a parse error. A build without the `shex` feature returns `METHOD_NOT_YET_SERVED` and never a fabricated report. | Test: `tests/shex_served.rs::parse_and_dispatch_failures_are_distinct` (L3) covers each case. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.7` | **Scoped reads.** Subsystem: RDF validation. Live validation reads the graph export that `GraphReadAuthority::project_core` returns under the universal served-read authority. Hidden rows and other graphs never reach the validator. Inline Turtle is caller data and reads no stored row. The call leaves graph rows and any stored schema unchanged. | Test: `tests/shex_served.rs::live_validation_obeys_read_authority` (L3) runs two principals and a second tenant and asserts hidden rows cause no difference. |
| `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R084.8` | **Evidence and hot-path proof.** Subsystem: RDF validation. Entry point: an authenticated served `ShexValidate` request. Caller chain: wire dispatch → `try_handle_shex_validation` in `src/server/handlers/rdf/dispatch.rs` → `handle_shex_validate` in `src/server/handlers/rdf/validation.rs` → `medulla_shex::validate`. The journey test covers inline and live data, ordered mixed outcomes, parse failures, unknown labels and denied scope. It also asserts the known matcher limits. `OneOf` takes the first matching branch. Shared predicates match greedily. The inverse flag is ignored. Depth above 40 assumes success. It records no claim of complete language support. | Test: `tests/shex_served.rs::served_shex_contract_journey` (L3). |
